3 · Other star labels

No exact or one-edit recurrence occurs in another star label. Within the frozen two-edit net, f68r3.X.2 is the only other astronomical label locus. No qualifying token occurs in the f68r1 or f68r2 S star-label sets. f67v1.Y.2 is radial text, not an isolated star label. A two-edit olchey reading also occurs at f80r.X.2, but that unit labels a bathing figure: H/U olchdy versus V olchey.

The broad net contains23 two-edit forms, including olchey at34 source loci across several sections. Those ordinary-looking body-text neighbours make a broad string family less specific, not a coherent set of repeated star names. No enrichment or common-meaning claim is made.

4 · Is there a historical name to test?

No specific historical name is currently justified for oalcheg. Its Aries figure/star has no independently identified individual referent. The nearby-string astronomical lead oalcheol sits under a curved connector. The star cluster itself has a different label: H/U doaro, V doary.

If the cluster is the Pleiades, it provides a conditional lexical family for doaro/doary. Isidore, Etymologiae III.71.13, uses the written forms Pliades and Vergilias (nominative lemma Vergiliae). This is edited primary textual attestation, not evidence for Voynich language, local fifteenth-century usage or pronunciation. The published seven-planets alternative remains open. Primary Latin text.

Alcyone is not selected: the image does not identify an individual Pleiad as owner of the word. Arabic al- star names are not selected from EVA oal- resemblance. Tail/dragon/occultation/lunar-event ideas remain unassigned comparison leads; the curved line does not distinguish them.

The useful next discrimination is graphical: find a dated diagram that independently matches the cluster–connector–moon arrangement, retaining label ownership. This could constrain whether the connector carries a relation/event term rather than a proper name. Fitting sounds now would assume the fact we need to establish.
